## Document 006: 10月金融数据点评：社融增速回升

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月金融数据点评：社融增速回升.pdf
Report source: 中证鹏元
Date: 2026-11-24
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

中证鹏元资信评估股份有限公司
研究发展部
赵云蛟
zhaoyj@cspengyuan.com
吴进辉
wujh@cspengyuan.com
主要内容：
11 月 13 日，央行公布 2026 年 10 月金融统计数据。总体来看，10 月社融存量增速
企稳回升，信贷总量持续承压，且单月新增规模转负，广义融资结构重塑趋势延续。
社融增量同比多增，主要依赖政府债券拉动，同时表外及直接融资边际改善，8,000
亿政策性金融工具投放带动相关配套资金加速落地，对委托贷款形成支撑。信贷结
构持续分化，微观主体加杠杆意愿依然偏弱。其中企业中长贷在新型政策性金融工
具集中投放支撑下维持相对韧性，而地产贴息政策效果尚未充分体现，居民端资产
负债表延续收缩。监管强化防范资金空转，大幅压降票据融资。货币与存款方面广
义流动性扩张呈现企稳迹象，前期政策资金陆续拨付至相关实体企业，带动企业活
期现金流改善并推升 M1 增速回升。存款端主要是流动性在季初跨部门转移，同时
政府债集中发行与季节性缴税推动财政存款扩张，对实体流动性产生一定的回笼效

应。
整体而言，10 月金融数据反映出资产负债表的自发修复与收缩进程仍在延续，实
体内生复苏动能尚待巩固，但在政策组合拳显效下，全年 GDP 增长目标预计可顺
利实现。叠加四季度权益市场大幅调整，市场避险情绪被进一步放大，微观主体整
体呈现防御收缩态势。政策层面，预计年内继续出台增量举措概率不高，主要依赖
存量政策效能发挥。后续需重点观察前期落地的地产贴息政策对房地产销售端的实
质效果，持续关注政策性金融工具转化为实物工作量后对企业中长期贷款的带动作
用。货币政策方面，政策利率换锚正式落地相当于释放了隐性降息效果，在配合跨
年流动性平稳过渡或者稳定权益市场等诉求下，仍存在降准的可能性。当前市场博
弈的核心将逐步转向对 2027 年的定调，重点关注 12 月的中央经济工作会议。
社融增速回升——10 月金融数据点评 宏观点评
2026 年 11 月 13 日

### Page 3

2026 年 11 月 13 日，央行公布 2026 年 10 月金融统计数据。
一、社融：10 月社融存量增速企稳回升，政府债融资依然是核心支撑
2026 年 10 月新增社融 1.14 万亿元，同比多增 3,251 亿元，社融存量增速较上月回升 0.1 个百分点至
7.2%。本月社融总量整体企稳回升，广义融资结构重塑趋势仍在延续。在信贷总量承压的背景下，社融增量
主要依赖政府债券拉动。直接融资累计占比达 53%，宏观融资大盘正逐步降低对传统表内信贷的单一依赖，
政策驱动下的融资结构转型特征持续显现。
分项数据来看，政府债券当月新增 1.18 万亿元，同比多增 6,983 亿元，政府债发行规模在 9 月冲高后
虽有所回落，但仍然同比多增，在化债与稳增长诉求下构成广义融资的核心增量。人民币贷款减少 5,846 亿
元，同比多减 5,692 亿元，实体信贷需求不足依然是社融的主要拖累项。表外及直接融资方面边际改善，委
托贷款当月新增 1,862 亿元，同比多增 208 亿元，10 月底，8,000 亿政策性金融工具全部投放，带动相关配

套资金加速落地，对委托贷款形成支撑。表外未贴现承兑汇票减少 1,687 亿元，同比少减 1,208 亿元，同时
企业债券新增 3,446 亿元，同比多增 946 亿元，在融资环境整体宽松及利率下行周期中实体企业直接债券融
资意愿整体回升。
图1 广义融资结构重塑延续，人民币信贷是主要拖累，政府债是主要支撑
资料来源：Wind，中证鹏元整理

二、信贷：10 月信贷总量承压，政策发力支撑企业中长端维持相对韧性
10 月新增人民币贷款减少 1,000 亿元，同比多减 3,200 亿元。信贷总量相对承压且各部门结构持续分
化，微观主体加杠杆意愿依然偏弱，资产负债表的自发修复与收缩进程仍在延续。当前信贷扩张的动能主要
依赖宏观政策的托底，实体经济内生性的投资与消费需求尚待进一步激发。
居民端信贷当月减少 4,300 亿元，同比多减 696 亿元。其中居民短贷减少 2,700 亿元，同比少减 166 亿
元，尽管 10 月已进入双十一大促阶段，且各地消费品以旧换新及相关贴息政策正陆续落地发力，但实际消
费信贷的整体回升态势依然偏弱，微观主体在收入预期不稳背景下对日常消费加杠杆的意愿普遍审慎，消
费信用扩张动能受到实质性制约。居民中长贷减少 1,563 亿元，同比多减 863 亿元，主要受制于房地产市场
销售端仍在底部调整区间，新增按揭贷款的派生规模有限，10 月开始实施的地产贴息政策对信贷数据的拉

动尚未充分体现，多重因素共同拖累居民端资产负债表扩张。
企业端贷款减少 1,600 亿元，同比多减 5,100 亿元，内部呈现长强短弱特征。企业中长贷新增 1,600 亿
元，同比多增 1,300 亿元，在信贷整体偏弱背景下表现出相对韧性，主要反映了政策端的支撑效果，新型政
策性金融工具在 9 月-10 月集中投放，为重大项目有效补充了资本金，叠加大规模设备更新等产业政策的结
构性引导，直接撬动了银行长端配套信贷的定向投放。企业短贷新增 100 亿元，同比多增 2,000 亿元，实体
企业常规营运资金需求边际企稳。票据融资大幅减少 3,117 亿元，同比多减 8,123 亿元，主要由于监管持续
强化防范资金空转套利，银行通过票据贴现单纯做大信贷规模的行为受到严格规范。近期市场关于票据剥
离信贷属性的预期有所升温，未来若相关统计口径优化落地，票据定价及业务模式将进一步脱离信贷扰动
并回归真实贸易本源。

三、货币与存款：10 月流动性季节性转移，财政存款扩张，M1 增速边际回升
10 月 M1 同比 4.4%，较 9 月回升 1.2 个百分点，M2 同比 7.1%，较 9 月微降 0.1 个百分点。同时社融
存量同比 7.2%，较上月回升 0.1 个百分点。整体看，10 月广义流动性扩张呈现企稳迹象，资金活化程度边
际改善，而存款端则表现出典型的季初跨部门转移及缴税抽水效应。
从货币供给走势看，M1 回升与 M2 微降带动 M2 与 M1 剪刀差由上月的 4.0%收窄至 2.7%。M2 偏弱主
要由表内信贷收缩导致存款派生机制受阻所致。M1 增速的回升主要来源于前期宏观政策资金逐步向下游传
导，9 月至 10 月集中投放的新型政策性金融工具资金以及前期发行的政府债券资金陆续拨付至相关实体企
业的项目账户，直接带动了企业活期现金流的边际改善。社融增速回升且与 M2 剪刀差间隔 10 个月转正至

0.1%，进一步确认广义流动性扩张的企稳态势。

### Page 8

存款方面，10 月新增人民币存款 1.21 万亿元，同比多增 6,000 亿元，主要是季初流动性跨部门转移。
居民存款减少 9,200 亿元，同比少减 4,200 亿元，企业存款减少 1.25 万亿元，同比多减 1,647 亿元。与实体
部门存款减少相对应，非银机构存款增加 1.58 万亿元，同比少增 2,700 亿元，非银资金规模的扩张主要受
季初理财资金回流非银体系拉动。此外 10 月财政存款增加 1.4 万亿元，同比多增 6,800 亿元，缴税大月，
叠加政府债集中发行导致财政存款账面大幅扩张，财政资金阶段性沉淀对实体流动性形成一定的回笼效应，
后续财政资金能否加速下拨将关系到 M1 增速回升的持续性。

总体来看，10 月社融存量增速企稳回升，信贷总量持续承压，且单月新增规模转负，广义融资结构重
塑趋势延续。社融增量同比多增，主要依赖政府债券拉动，同时表外及直接融资边际改善，8,000 亿政策性
金融工具投放带动相关配套资金加速落地，对委托贷款形成支撑。信贷结构持续分化，微观主体加杠杆意愿
依然偏弱。其中企业中长贷在新型政策性金融工具集中投放支撑下维持相对韧性，而地产贴息政策效果尚
未充分体现，居民端资产负债表延续收缩。监管强化防范资金空转，大幅压降票据融资。货币与存款方面广
义流动性扩张呈现企稳迹象，前期政策资金陆续拨付至相关实体企业，带动企业活期现金流改善并推升 M1
增速回升。存款端主要是流动性在季初跨部门转移，同时政府债集中发行与季节性缴税推动财政存款扩张，
对实体流动性产生一定的回笼效应。
整体而言，10 月金融数据反映出资产负债表的自发修复与收缩进程仍在延续，实体内生复苏动能尚待
巩固，但在政策组合拳显效下，全年 GDP 增长目标预计可顺利实现。叠加四季度权益市场大幅调整，市场

避险情绪被进一步放大，微观主体整体呈现防御收缩态势。政策层面，预计年内继续出台增量举措概率不
高，主要依赖存量政策效能发挥。后续需重点观察前期落地的地产贴息政策对房地产销售端的实质效果，持
续关注政策性金融工具转化为实物工作量后对企业中长期贷款的带动作用。货币政策方面，政策利率换锚
正式落地相当于释放了隐性降息效果，在配合跨年流动性平稳过渡或者稳定权益市场等诉求下，仍存在降
准的可能性。当前市场博弈的核心将逐步转向对 2027 年的定调，重点关注 12 月的中央经济工作会议。

## Document 021: 2026-11-11_［みずほ経済インサイト］堅調なAI需要で大幅増益が視野_1ea94428

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-11-11_［みずほ経済インサイト］堅調なAI需要で大幅増益が視野_1ea94428.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

堅調なAI 需要で大幅増益が視野 
投資増につながるかが焦点に

○ 製造業の業績見通しは大幅に改善している。輸出向けを中心とするAI関連財の需要拡大が背景。
2026年度は好業績が期待され、二桁増益が視野に入る 
○ AI関連財の増産を背景に、国内製造業の設備不足感は2025年後半以降強まっている。足元では関
連業種の機械受注が増加しており、AI需要拡大が国内設備投資に波及する兆しがみられる 
○ 設備投資は緩やかな拡大が息長く続く見通し。ただし、米国でデータセンター建設規制が実施さ
れれば、輸出・業績・設備投資の好循環が逆回転に転じるリスクも

１．企業景況感は AI 関連の製造業を中心に改善 
AIの学習・利用に必要なデータセンター投資や半導体需要が世界的に堅調であることを背景に、日
本の製造業の景況感は幅広く改善している。日銀短観（2026年9月調査）では、大企業・製造業の業況
判断DIが6月調査から改善した（図表1）。業種別では、半導体や電子部品などを含む電気機械や、半
導体製造装置を含む生産用機械の改善が際立つ。そのほか、窯業・土石製品、非鉄金属、半導体材料・
素材を扱う化学などでも改善がみられた。ロイター短観（10月調査）でも、「半導体関連市場の需要
が強い」との声が散見され、AI関連需要の堅調さがうかがえる。 
AI関連財の輸出が拡大し、足元で企業の業績見通しは大きく改善している。企業業績の改善を国内
の需要拡大につなげるうえで、次の焦点となるのが設備投資である。企業業績の改善に比べて、投資

### Page 2

2

などの内需への恩恵はまだ明確ではなく、今後設備投資の増加として発現する可能性が期待される。 
２．AI 関連財輸出は好調。2026 年度の製造業は二桁増益が視野に 
世界的なAIサービスの普及に伴い、データセンターや関連機器への投資が拡大している。大規模な
データセンターを保有・運営し、クラウドサービスやAI向けの計算基盤を提供するIT企業、とりわけ
ハイパースケーラーと呼ばれる大規模事業者を中心に、高水準の設備投資を継続する見通しが示され
ていることを踏まえると、AI関連投資の高い伸びは当面持続する見込みである。こうしたなか、モデ
ルの演算を担う処理装置のほか、大量のデータを蓄える記憶装置、サーバー同士をつなぐ通信ケーブ
ル、電力を安定供給する電源装置などといった財において投資需要が高まっている。日本企業が供給
する財についても、電子部品・機器への需要が増加しているほか、AI向け半導体の増産投資は日本の
半導体製造・検査装置や材料への需要を押し上げている。 
図表2は、輸出全体から、WTOの定義に従ってAI関連財を抽出したものだ。AI関連財輸出は2025年終
盤以降加速しており、8月時点では前年比＋29.1％と高い伸びになった。主因は半導体・電子部品で、
旺盛な需要のもとで半導体価格が大幅に上昇し、輸出金額を押し上げている。また、半導体製造・検
査機器や、データセンターに組み込まれる機械・部材、半導体の製造に用いられる原材料・素材など、
幅広いAI関連財が輸出増に寄与している。 
輸出額の高い伸びを受け、製造業では業績見通しが改善している。日銀短観（9月調査）の2026年度
経常利益計画（全規模・製造業）は、6月調査時点から大幅に上方修正され、前年度比＋11.6％の増益
が見込まれる（図表3）。特に電気機械では、上期の堅調な輸出実績を反映して、経常利益計画が大き
く上方修正された。このほか、業況判断DIの改善が目立った業種でも同様の傾向がみられた。堅調な
AI関連需要が下期も続けば、製造業では大幅増益が期待される。

３．AI 関連業種の投資需要は増勢加速の兆しも 
今後の実質GDPへの影響を見通すうえでの焦点は、企業収益の拡大が設備投資を中心とした内需の押
し上げにどの程度繋がるかだ。この点、AI需要の追い風を受け好業績が見込まれることを踏まえれば、
設備投資の伸びはやや物足りなさが否めない。日銀短観（9月調査）によると、製造業の2026年度設備
投資計画は直近5年間では最も低い伸びに留まっている（図表4）。建材や生産設備の価格上昇、金利
上昇に伴う資金調達コストの増加が投資負担となり、好業績のもとでも計画の積み増しを抑えている
可能性がある。 
もっとも、設備投資需要は計画対比で伸び余地がある。AI関連財を中心に増産が進むなか、能力増
強投資の必要性が高まっているためだ。AI関連財の生産は2025年秋以降、はっきりとした増加傾向が
続いている（図表5）。生産・営業用設備判断DI（全規模・製造業）も2025年後半から低下しており、
製造業の設備不足感は強まっている（図表6）。 
実際、AI需要の拡大が今後の設備投資を押し上げる兆しもみられる。図表7は、設備投資の先行指標
とされる機械受注（国内需要向け）を需要者別に分解し、価格変動の影響を取り除いたものだ。これ
をみると2025年夏場以降、情報通信機械、電気機械、はん用・生産用機械といった業種からの受注が
急増していることがわかる。結果として、半導体製造装置をはじめとするAI・データセンター関連品
目では受注残高が積み上がっている（図表8）。 
ただし、機械受注が設備投資として顕在化するまでにはタイムラグを伴うとみられる。そもそも、
受注残高が積み上がっている背景には、旺盛な需要だけでなく、供給面の制約による影響があると考
えられる1。このため、設備投資の増加ペースは緩やかなものにとどまる公算が大きいとみているが、
その分、今後は息の長い投資拡大局面が続くだろう。

図表 5 鉱工業生産 図表6 生産・営業用設備判断DI

（注） AI関連はWTOによるAI-enabling productsの定義に準ずる

### Page 4

4

４．今後は政府の投資支援策も民間投資を後押し。米国の規制強化リスクには要注意 
以上のように、先行きの設備投資は、設備不足感の強まるAI関連産業の投資需要に支えられ、緩や
かに増勢を強めると見込まれる。 
また今後は、政府の政策支援が、投資コストの負担軽減を通じて、設備投資を後押しするとみられ
る。7月末に始動した政府の「大胆な投資促進税制」では、一定の要件を満たす国内投資について、即
時償却や税額控除の適用を認める減税措置が設けられた。さらに、2027年度予算の編成では、特別予
算枠として『「強く豊かな日本」投資枠』を設け、民間投資を促す支援策が検討されている。具体的
な支援策が整えば、複数年度にわたる投資計画の実行を支えよう。 
なお、11月3日に投開票された米国中間選挙の結果を受け、米国におけるデータセンター投資が下振
れるリスクにも触れておきたい。同選挙では、データセンター建設計画の多いテキサス州やジョージ
ア州の州知事選などにおいて、建設の一時停止を掲げる民主党候補が勝利した。米国では、データセ
ンター建設規制に関して州政府や地方自治体が大きな権限を持つ。 
実際、市場ではAI関連企業の業績・投資下振れが意識され、グローバルにハイテク銘柄の株価が急
落した。仮に今後、米国でデータセンター建設が規制されれば、日本でも半導体・電子部品や製造装
置などの輸出が減少し、企業業績が下押しされる可能性がある。その際、AI関連財が減産に転じて設
備不足感が弱まれば、日本国内における能力増強投資の需要も後退しかねない。新知事の任期開始お
よび新議会の招集は2027年1月だが、当面の米国政治動静については注意深くみていく必要があろう。

1 なお、供給制約に起因して、資本財の輸入浸透度（国内供給に占める輸入品の割合）が一層上昇する可能性には留意が必要で
ある。今後、国内の供給余力が限られるなかで投資需要が一層輸入品に向かえば、国産資本財への需要が減退し、投資需要増に
よる GDP の押し上げ効果が限られるリスクがある。

多重利空夾擊，全球 AI 股連日重挫
2026 年 11 月 20 日
一、市場動態
1. 美股 11/19 反彈遭逢賣壓：Anthropic 19 日於那斯達克掛牌，募資約 813 億美元創史
上最大 IPO，開盤大漲 34%、盤中市值一度突破 2 兆美元，終場漲幅收斂至 17.1%，
然激勵效果未能擴散至其他 AI 股，反被視為「利多出盡」的獲利了結訊號。美股四大
指數開高走低、全數重挫逾 2%：道瓊下跌 2.19%，為 6 月以來首度跌破 50,000 點；
標普 500 跌 2.02%、連六黑，逾 460 檔成分股收黑；那斯達克跌 2.25%、費半跌 2.41%，

VIX 盤中一度升近 29、創 3 月以來新高。個股方面，輝達早盤一度上漲 4.7%後翻黑，
收跌 3.3%，本月累計跌幅約 28%；AMD 遭放空機構 Hunterbrook 指控與受美國制裁
的中國伺服器業者往來，重挫 7.72%，高度依賴舉債擴張的 CoreWeave、Nebius 分
別重挫 6.1%與 9.4%。Fed 官員密集釋出鷹派訊號，警告 AI 建設熱潮恐推升通膨，市
場預估 12 月升息機率約 96%；初領失業金人數亦高於預期，增添景氣疑慮。
2. 亞股 11/20 續挫，台股同步承壓：亞股 11/20 早盤延續美股走勢，賣壓擴及亞洲 AI
硬體供應鏈，日經 225 與韓國 KOSPI 同步重挫逾 4%。台股 19 日下跌 1,768.02 點，
收 38,426.89 點，跌幅 4.40%，創史上收盤第 7 大跌點後，20 日再受美股下挫拖累，
加權指數持續走低，截至台灣時間下午 1 點 22 分為止，台股加權指數下挫 6.80%；台
積電、聯發科、台達電等權值股同步重挫。

4. Anthropic 史上最大 IPO 引發資金排擠與「利多出盡」情緒：Anthropic 募資約 813
億美元，盤中市值一度突破 2 兆美元，但美股開高走低、晶片股由紅翻黑，市場將其視
為 AI 交易階段性高點的獲利了結訊號。市場亦解讀，大型 AI 模型公司直接上市，讓資
金可直接押注 AI 應用端，相對降低透過硬體供應鏈間接參與的需求，並在短期內吸納
部分 AI 配置資金。
5. 台股籌碼面賣壓與融資斷頭形成負向循環：外資 11 月累計賣超達 8,303 億元，台指期
淨空單維持約 9.9 萬口；台股跌破年線後融資維持率偏低，斷頭與停損賣壓同步出籠，
新台幣亦貶至 33 元附近，加重外資匯損考量。金管會雖自 20 日起實施限空令 2.0（個

股收跌逾 3.5%者隔日禁止平盤下放空、盤中借券賣出上限由 3%降至 1%），但短線仍
難完全抵銷被動賣壓。
資料來源：臺灣證券交易所、元大銀行整理
三、市場看法
1. AI 需求尚未反轉，但產業正由資本支出驅動轉向獲利驗證：我們認為，判斷 AI 產業是
否出現結構性反轉，關鍵應在於終端運算需求、CSP 資本支出及供應鏈訂單是否持續下

### Page 4

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
降，而非單純觀察股價跌幅。輝達第三季資料中心營收年增 110%，且 Vera Rubin 平
台已進入出貨階段，顯示先進運算設備需求仍具有實質支撐。此外，Reuters 引述 LSEG
資料指出，輝達財報公布後，分析師對下一財年調整後淨利的平均預估由 3,800 億美元
上修至 3,930 億美元，但股價評價反而轉趨保守。這項變化反映市場並未全面否定輝達
的獲利能力，而是開始要求更高的風險補償。我們認為，若未來 CSP 未大幅縮減資本
支出，AI 晶片與相關供應鏈仍具有中長期成長基礎；但若資本支出成長持續快於營收

及現金流改善，科技股評價仍可能承壓。
2. AI 信用風險仍較集中於高槓桿業者，尚未顯示全面性信用危機：目前市場對 AI 企業融
資風險的擔憂逐漸升高，但不同企業的財務承受能力存在明顯差異。大型 CSP 通常擁
有成熟的雲端、廣告或軟體業務，可透過營業現金流支應部分 AI 投資；相較之下，部
分新興資料中心業者高度依賴債務、設備融資或外部股權資金，對利率與資本市場環境
更加敏感。10 月 FOMC 會議紀錄指出，投資級公司債利差僅小幅擴大，且以歷史標準
衡量仍相對偏低；高收益債利差則擴大較明顯，尤其與資料中心融資有關的債券受到更
大衝擊。這顯示市場正在提高部分高風險企業的融資門檻，但尚不足以判定信用壓力已
全面擴散至整體投資級債市場。我們認為，後續仍應觀察相關企業的債務到期結構、再
融資需求與信用利差變化，判斷風險是否進一步擴大。
3. 科技股次產業將進一步分化：隨著市場由追逐 AI 投資規模轉向評估投資效益，科技次
產業未來表現可能明顯分歧。晶圓代工與先進封裝產業具有較高技術門檻，仍可受惠先

進運算晶片需求；HBM 及高階記憶體則具備 AI 伺服器成長題材，但其獲利仍需觀察供
需循環及價格變化；半導體設備業者則較容易受到客戶建廠時程及資本支出調整影響。
另一方面，部分尚未建立穩定營收模式的 AI 基礎建設業者，可能因融資成本與投資回
收壓力，面臨更大的評價修正。我們認為，科技股中期並非全面轉空，而是市場可能更
偏好具備技術競爭優勢、穩定訂單、獲利成長及自由現金流的企業。未來即使 AI 產業
整體維持成長，也不代表所有相關公司的營收、獲利及股價都能同步受惠。
4. Fed 政策仍有不確定性，但企業獲利成長可望部分抵銷評價壓力：目前 Fed 升息預期
已對全球科技股評價形成明顯壓力，但未來市場方向不僅取決於 12 月是否升息，更重
要的是升息幅度與後續政策路徑是否超出市場預期。然而，美國經濟與企業獲利目前尚
未出現全面性衰退跡象，若未來企業營收與獲利持續成長，即使本益比難以回到過去高
檔，仍有助於股市透過基本面改善逐步修復跌幅。我們認為，Fed 政策風險不容忽視，
但也不宜單憑升息預期就認定股市中長期多頭結構已全面改變。

5. 台股短線仍需消化籌碼壓力，但企業獲利成長仍可提供中期支撐：若以台股走勢來看，
其近期跌幅明顯超過部分國際市場，我們認為除受到美股科技股修正影響，也與大型電
子權值股集中、外資調節及短線市場流動性下降有關。不過，根據金管會公布數據，截
至 2026 年 10 月底，國內上市櫃公司累計營收約 56.75 兆元，年增 39.05%；前三季
累計稅前淨利約 6.61 兆元，年增 86.43%，顯示整體企業獲利仍具成長支撐。此外，
金管會自 11 月 20 日起擴大限空措施，對收盤跌幅逾 3.5%的個股實施次日平盤下放空
限制，並將盤中借券賣出數量上限由 3%降至 1%。我們認為，政策措施有助降低部分

短線賣壓，但市場真正止穩仍需觀察外資流向、融資去槓桿是否趨緩，以及主要電子權

6. 後續觀察重點：（1）美國下週公布核心 PCE 物價指數，市場預期年增 3.2%，將影響
12 月升息幅度；（2）12 月 FOMC 升息 1 碼或 2 碼，以及 2027 年升息路徑；（3）輝
達新任執行長人選與黃仁勳赴參議院作證情況，牽動出口管制後續走向；（4）BIS 新規
執行細節及東南亞資料中心許可審查進度。
四、 投資建議
1. 短線嚴控槓桿與部位集中度，選股邏輯轉向「現金流與訂單能見度」優先：我們認為，

AI 產業成長動能尚未確認反轉，但市場已不再接受單純依靠資本支出擴張支撐評價。
前述利空因素短期內恐難完全消除或明朗，市場短線仍將隨著消息面的發展而變化。我
們預期後續投資人將更重視科技股的訂單能見度、企業獲利、資產負債表及自由現金
流。同時，台灣在先進製程、先進封裝及 AI 伺服器領域仍具競爭優勢，中期基本面展
望維持偏正向。然而，近期指數連續重挫，反映市場流動性與籌碼壓力尚未解除，短期
仍須觀察外資賣壓、融資水位及美股科技股走勢。投資人宜採取分批及分散方式參與中
長期產業成長，同時避免過度集中於融資需求高、獲利能見度較低的 AI 企業。
2. 以股債平衡穿越波動，固定收益「先短後長」：Fed 仍處升息循環，長天期債券價格仍
有波動風險，但美國公債目前具有高殖利率吸引力與防禦價值，建議以中短天期美國公
債、高信評投資等級債及美元貨幣型商品鎖定相對高檔的收益，待升息循環接近尾聲再
逐步拉長存續期間。投資級債則受惠相對穩健的信用品質，惟仍須關注 AI 相關企業信
用風險是否進一步擴散。

30%。公司表示，面對 DRAM 等零組件漲價逆風，第三季 PC 營

Gaming、高階 AI PC 及商用 PC，佔 PC 營收比重超過 65%，且
商用 PC 出貨量維持強勁，年成長超過 30%。另外，伺服器業務
方面，儘管部分訂單遞延至第四季認列，第三季伺服器營收仍實
現超過 100%的年增長；通用型 CPU 伺服器亦受惠於企業擴建地
端資料中心以支援 AI 工作負載，營收年增超過 80%。獲利方面，

單季稅後 EPS 29.1 元。

公司 2025 產品營收比重：System 52%、Open Platform 28%、ISG 
18%、AIOT 2%。銷售地區比重：歐洲 30%、亞 太 48%、美 洲 22%。
在筆電部分，公司主要競爭廠商為 Apple、HP、DELL、宏碁、微
星、技嘉、聯想集團等。

二.產業趨勢概況 
1.3Q26 全球 PC 出貨量暴跌逾二成，AI 搶走記憶體成衰退元兇 
受 AI 資料中心擴張排擠記憶體與零組件供給影響，2026 年第三
季全球 PC 市場旺季不旺，根據 IDC，3Q26 PC 單季出貨量降至
6,270 萬台，年減 20.1%。由於 DRAM 與 SSD 價格暴漲逾 4 倍，
在 PC 成本占比由 15%飆升至近 40%，加上處理器與顯示卡等元
件短缺，以及業者於上半年提前拉貨透支需求，導致整體市場面
臨沉重的庫存與成本壓力。就各大品牌來看，第三季普遍陷入雙
位數的衰退，聯想第三季出貨量年減-22.6%、惠普-30.9%、戴爾
-25%、蘋果-11.3%，而華碩則展現出強勁的韌性，第三季出貨量
達 550 萬台，年減 8.6%，為全球前五大 PC 品牌中唯一跌幅控制
在個位數的業者，表現明顯優於其他競爭對手。然而研究機構仍

2. 明年 CSP Capex 預估破 1.5 兆美元，AI 伺服器產值年增逾 9 成 
根據集邦科技(TrendForce)最新研究，受惠於 Google、Amazon、
Meta、Microsoft、Oracle 及中國四大科技巨頭等全球前九大雲端
服務供應商(CSP)擴大投資 AI 基礎設施，預估 2026 年九大 CSP
資本支出總額將逾 9,570 億美元，YoY +106%，2027 年更因伺服
器整機漲價、零組件缺貨漲價及供應商要求預付款等因素，有望
突破 1.5 兆美元，YoY +60%，雙雙優於預期。在超大型 CSP、Tier-2
資料中心與主權 AI 等需求帶動下，預估 2026 年 AI 伺服器產值約
4,790 億美元，占整體伺服器產值近 75%；2027 年隨輝達 GB300、
VR200 與超微 Helios 等整櫃式方案成為主流，加上記憶體等關鍵
零組件短缺推升單價，預計整體伺服器產值將首度跨越 1 兆美元，
其中 AI 伺服器產值將躍升至近 9,200 億美元 YoY +90%，占比進
一步提高至 81%左右。

3. 2026 年全球 AI 伺服器出貨量預計年增 31% 
根據 TrendForce 最新 AI server 產業研究，近期超大型 CSP、
Tier-2 資料中心業者對 NVIDIA GB/VR 等整櫃式 AI server 方案的
採購意願明顯提高，且 Google、AWS 新一代 ASIC 平台於 2026
下半年陸續放量，加上中系業者擴大採用本地 AI 方案以滿足雲端
大型語言模型(LLM) AI 需求，促使 TrendForce 上調 2026 年 AI 
server 出貨年成長幅度，從原先的 28%更新為近 31%。北美五大
業者 2026 年的資本支出多翻倍提升，反映出大型 CSP 廠持續擴
建 AI 資料中心、GPU 叢集、液冷散熱等新一代基礎設施，以應對
生成式 AI 及大型模型快速成長帶來的算力需求。其中，Google 
2026 至 2027 年都將側重自家 TPU 晶片發展，2027 年出貨量有
望繼續高速成長。AWS 2026 年以 NVIDIA GB300 作為 GPU AI 
server 主力，自研 ASIC 也穩定出貨，預期至 2027 年量能將再擴
大。Meta 2026 年主要採用 NVIDIA GB/VR、AMD Helios 整櫃式
方案，自研 ASIC 布局預計至 2027 年將明顯加速，為該公司長期
降低 AI 基礎設施成本、提升推論效率的重要策略。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

一. 4Q26 伺服器遞延訂單放量抵銷 PC 逆風 
公司公告 10 月營收 1076 億元，MoM +10.7%，YoY +62.2%。今
年累計營收 8500 億，YoY +41.1%。展望 4Q26，受惠於伺服器
呈現強勁成長，公司預估 4Q26 伺服器營收季增 100%，年增
280%，主要由於部分資料中心客戶於第三季因建置時程延後，使
伺服器出貨與營收遞延至第四季認列。而 PC 與零組件業務方面，
受到記憶體等零組件價格上漲推升終端 ASP 影響，壓抑終端市場
需求，公司預估 4Q26 電腦業務營收季減 15%，年增 15%。零組
件則預估將季減 10%，年減 15%。預估公司 4Q26 營收 3106 億

二. 預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 89.30/86.35 元 
展望 2026 年，在伺服器業務方面，公司表示目前整體訂單能見度
強勁，除了受惠於 GPU AI 伺服器需求暢旺之外，企業擴建地端資
料中心，亦帶動通用型 CPU 伺服器需求同步提升。在第四季翻倍
季增帶動下，公司預估 2026 全年伺服器營收達成至少 150%的年
成長目標。而 PC 與零組件業務，則受記憶體等零組件價格大幅
上漲影響，在 4Q26 至 1H27 的 PC 市場需求逆風與價格成本混亂
期間，PC 業務的獲利能力與營業利益率將承受一定壓力。預估公
司 2026 全年營收 1.05 兆，YoY +42.5%，毛利率 15.8%，營利率
7.2%，稅後淨利 663 億，稅後 EPS 89.30 元。展望 2027 年，PC
整體市場受零組件價格上漲與需求定價混亂影響，公司預估 2027
年全球 PC 市場整體出貨量將年減 10%至 15%，就季度節奏來看，
上半年跌幅較大，下半年將逐步回升。而伺服器業務方面，管理
層則預估 2027 年伺服器營收可望維持雙位數至高雙位數的年增
率。由於公司客戶涵蓋 Tier 2/3 CSP、NeoCloud、大型科技公司、
AI 研究機構、企業地端資料中心及國家級主權 AI 專案，儘管近期
市場面臨資本市場融資條件趨緊以及美國期中大選後各地資料中
心建置監管趨嚴等逆風，但目前訂單需求並未出現明顯延遲或變
化。預估公司 2027 全年營收 1.26 兆，YoY +19.5%，毛利率
14.5%，營利率 6.6%，稅後淨利 641 億，稅後 EPS 86.35 元。

強茂(2481 TT) Buy

車用市佔擴大與 AI 伺服器拉貨帶動，投資建議 Buy。  
投資建議：展望未來，隨著車用市佔率持續擴大，加上雲端資
料中心與 AI 伺服器新世代平台功率元件拉貨需求攀升，給予 
Buy 的投資建議，目標價 160 元。 
車用市佔擴大與 AI 伺服器拉貨，3Q26 獲利三率三升：  
強茂 3Q26 營收 43.50 億元（QoQ +10.0%、 YoY +33.0%），主
因車用及 AI 需求帶動功率元件需求轉強；毛利率因產品組合
優化提升至 37.3%（ QoQ +4.1 ppts、 YoY +6.0 ppts）；營業利益 
6.97 億元（QoQ +50.1%、 YoY +152.3%）；稅後淨利 6.49 億
元（QoQ +33.0%、 YoY +114.0%），3Q26 EPS 為 1.70 元。 
產品組合車用比重提升，產能佈局強化東南亞供應： 
YTD 3Q26 終端應用營收比重以車用 35%最高（YOY+2ppts），
車用產品營收較去年同期大幅成長 47.2%。AI 與雲端運算營收
佔整體營收逾 12%，拉貨將逐步提升。集團擁有三座晶圓廠及
五座後段封測廠，隨著 2026 年下半年越南胡志明市新封測廠加
入營運，進一步強化東南亞供應能力。預估 2026 年營收 
160.48 億元（YoY +22.6%），EPS 為 5.47 元。 
車用市佔率持續擴大，MOSFET 業務加速放量與定價能力提
升：隨著 AI 伺服器機櫃功耗提高，強茂成功切入美系 CSP 的  
ASIC 伺服器與 AI 機櫃電源供應鏈，主攻 Hot-Swap（熱插拔）
與 ORing（冗餘電源管理兩大關鍵應用），推動 AI 佔比朝 15%
以上邁進。車用方面，除了 40V 車規中低壓 MOSFET 持續放
量外，更推出碳化矽（SiC）MOSFET 搶攻日歐美市場。綜上所
述，預估強茂 2027 年營收 192.31 億元（YoY +19.8%），EPS 
8.41 元。基於車用市佔擴大與 AI 伺服器新世代平台拉貨需求攀
升，給予 Buy 投資建議，目標價 160 元。

2 群益投顧 
  ESG：  
在  ESG 永續發展表現上，強茂積極落實環境保護、供應鏈管理與綠色技術
創新三大面向： 
環境永續：持續投入節能減碳與能源轉型，透過製程節能改善及再生能源建
置，2025 年全年節電約 91.1 萬度，減少約 432 公噸 CO₂e 排放；太陽能系
統全年發電量突破 263 萬度，穩健提升綠電使用比重並朝向低碳營運邁進。
責任供應鏈：依循責任商業聯盟(RBA)標準深化供應鏈管理，年度完成 75 家
供應商 RBA 稽核且合格率達 100%，攜手合作夥伴打造具韌性的永續價值
鏈。 
創新與產品發展：持續深耕高效率功率半導體研發，2025 年集團累計有效
專利達 102 件，並維持 90 分以上的客戶滿意度，以高效能半導體解決方案
協助終端客戶提升能源使用效率，共同響應全球淨零碳排趨勢。

公司簡介： 
強茂成立於 1986 年，為台灣功率半導體領導廠商之一，主要從事離散元件
（ Discrete）之研發、製造與銷售，產品涵蓋 MOSFET、Schottky、Rectifier、
TVS、 Zener、 Bridge Transistor、 ESD Protection 及  SiC 等。公司母公司專
注於核心元件開發，並透過關係企業與子公司進行垂直與水平整合，包含熒
茂光學、凡甲科技、虹冠電子、汎星半導體及新貿科技。強茂在低功率矽整
流器領域位居全球第四大供應商，並持續聚焦車用電子、雲端資料中心與 AI
伺服器等高附加價值應用。 
主要競爭對手分析 
1. 國際一線 IDM 大廠（高階車用與 AI 伺服器主要對手） 
強茂近年積極搶攻歐美車廠與 AI 機櫃市佔，主要在以下領域與國際巨頭
正面交鋒（並受惠於供應鏈去中化或對手產能受限之轉單效應）： 
• 安世半導體（Nexperia）：最直接的車用與小訊號元件競爭對手。安
世在車用小訊號二極體、ESD/TVS 保護元件及中低壓 MOSFET 市
佔率極高；每當安世出現產能吃緊或地緣政治/出口管制干擾時，歐美
Tier 1 車廠往往優先將訂單轉移至強茂。 
• 意法半導體（STMicroelectronics） 、 安森美（onsemi） 、 英飛凌
（ Infineon） ：全球車用與工業功率半導體前三大巨頭，在車規
MOSFET、SiC（碳化矽）及 AI 伺服器電源管理擁有完整生態系。強
茂以高性價比及交期彈性切入其二供（Second Source）體系。 
• 威世（Vishay）、達爾（Diodes Incorporated）、羅姆（ROHM）：
在整流二極體、蕭特基二極體及中低壓 MOSFET 產品線高度重疊，
是強茂在美系與日系客戶端的主要比價與份額競爭者。 
2. 台灣分離式元件同業 
• 台半（5425）：在整流二極體與車用 MOSFET 布局深厚（切入 Tier

領域重疊度最高。 
• 朋程（8255）：專注車用發電機二極體（全球市佔第一）及高效能二

與強茂具備相似的客戶群。 
• 富鼎（8261）、杰力（5299）、大中（6435）：台灣純 MOSFET 設

與強茂在消費性電子及部分電源應用市場競爭。

車用市佔擴大與 AI 伺服器拉貨，3Q26 獲利三率三升： 
強茂 3Q26 營收 43.50 億元，QoQ+10.0%，YoY+33.0%，主因車用及 AI 需求

### Page 4

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 16 日

4 群益投顧 
帶動功率元件需求轉強；毛利率因產品組合優化提升至 37.3% ，
QoQ+4.1ppts，YoY+6.0ppts；營業利益 6.97 億元，QoQ+50.1%，YoY+152.3%，
呈現大幅提升；稅後淨利 6.49 億元，QoQ+32.96%， YoY+113.95%， 3Q26 
EPS 1.70 元，3Q26 財報表現亮眼。 
產品組合車用比重提升，產能佈局強化東南亞供應： 
YTD 3Q26 終端應用營收比重以車用 35%最高，年增 2 ppts，車用產品營收
較去年同期大幅成長 47.2%。 AI 與雲端運算營收佔整體營收逾 12%，受惠
新世代平台規格推進拉貨穩健。集團擁有三座晶圓廠及五座後段封測廠，隨
2026 年下半年越南胡志明市新封測廠加入營運，進一步強化東南亞第三供
應能力，2H26 營運持續增長，4Q26 營運預估與 3Q26 持平，預估 2026 年
營收 160.48 億元(YOY+22.56%)， EPS 為 5.47 元。 
車用市佔率持續擴大，MOSFET 業務加速放量與定價能力提升： 
強茂本身具有歐美車廠供應鏈能力，在安世半導體產能與出貨受阻後，客戶
端為確保供貨安全，加速將小訊號二極體與保護元件（ESD/TVS）訂單轉移
至強茂，使推升其車用營收比重與整體市佔率。隨著 AI 伺服器機櫃功耗
大幅提高，資料中心對供電穩定度與電源轉換效率的要求越發嚴苛。在成功
切入美系 CSP（雲端服務大廠）的 ASIC 伺服器與 AI 機櫃電源供應鏈後，
強茂主攻 Hot-Swap（熱插拔）與 ORing（冗餘電源管理）兩大關鍵應用。這
類高電流供電路徑需要具備「超低導通電阻（Low RDS(on)）」的中高壓
MOSFET 來降低功耗與發熱，其 ASP 遠高於傳統消費性元件，預估 AI 佔比
可朝 15%以上邁進。 
車用展望方面，目前佔強茂整體營收比重約 35%。隨著電動車與智慧座艙滲
透率提升，車內馬達驅動、電池管理系統（BMS）及車載充電器對 MOSFET 
的用量呈倍數增長。強茂除了既有的 40V 車規中低壓 MOSFET 持續放量
外，更進一步推出碳化矽（SiC）MOSFET 搶攻日本與歐美車用市場，產品
線從小訊號保護元件成功延伸至高毛利的動力與電源核心。綜上所述，預估
強茂 2027 年營收 192.31 億元(YOY+19.8%)， EPS 8.41 元。 
投資建議：展望未來，隨著車用市佔率持續擴大，加上雲端資料中心與 AI 
伺服器新世代平台功率元件拉貨需求攀升，給予 Buy 的投資建議，目標價
160 元。

個股報告

8 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

could be better than feared, especially for top-tier companies in

Greater China Technology Hardware

Industry View In-Line
Recent Research:                  Global Technology and
Telecom: 2026 AlphaWise Smartphone Survey:
Another Down Year That's Not Yet Priced In (16
Nov 2026)
While we believe some investors are turning more bearish on AI amid top-down
uncertainty, bottom-up discussions with supply chain companies suggest the
outlook for 2027 is better than feared. Within the AI universe, our key
recommendations include: Innolight, Largan, Wistron, Hon Hai, Wiwynn, Unimicron,

EMC, Lenovo, Delta, Bizlink and Huaqin.
For non-AI-driven consumer electronics companies, we believe any slower memory
price increases could provide a positive catalyst. Companies under our coverage
supplying components to Apple appear better positioned to deliver growth in 2027,
in our view, while some Android suppliers we spoke with anticipate potentially
weaker volumes. Our key recommendations include Xiaomi, Luxshare-H, Crystal and
Transsion.

AI servers, optical interconnects and infrastructure
- Hon Hai – AI demand remains strong; additional rack assembly share expected.
Management reiterated 30%+ AI demand growth in 2027, with no recent
deterioration. ASICs should remain approximately 20% of cloud revenue, as higher
VR200 rack prices offset faster ASIC growth. Hon Hai expects to gain rack assembly
share through the addition of one hyperscaler and one neocloud customer.
- Zhongji Innolight – Most 2027 contracts have confirmed volumes and prices, with

some customers providing 2028 guidance. Customers have increased 1.6T orders,
partly offset by lower 800G orders, with no backlog cancellations. NPO demand
has strengthened; volume production is scheduled for 2H27, with stronger growth in
2028. Annual transceiver price reductions remain normal, but silicon photonics,
product upgrades and a more efficient one-laser-to-four-lanes solution should
support margins.
Continued on the next page.

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
Innolight, Largan, Wistron, Hon Hai, Wiwynn, Unimicron, Lenovo, Delta, Bizlink
and Huaqin are our preferred stocks for the AI theme.
We believe Xiaomi, Luxshare-H, Crystal and Transsion are likely to enjoy a
gradual recovery over the following quarters.

### Page 3

M
Update
Morgan Stanley Research 3
Summary of Our Summit Feedback and Links to Individual
Company Reports
- Quanta – Improving AI product mix supports profitability; notebooks remain weak. Non-
NVIDIA projects should contribute a double-digit percentage of AI server revenue in 2027,
up from single digits in 2026, and generally carry higher margins. Quanta is negotiating
additional GPU consignment arrangements, although most remain unfinalized. Neocloud

exposure is limited to a single-digit percentage of total revenue.
- Huaqin – China SuperPoD deployments offer a multi-year growth opportunity. Volume
deliveries to two major Chinese cloud service providers began in 2H26 and should expand
to three in 2027. Huaqin has completed development of a 3.2T NPO system solution, with
a 6.4T solution under development, and plans to begin customer promotion in 2027.
- Bizlink – The company believes >20% sequential revenue growth in 4Q26 is achievable,

supported by organic growth and Interplex. Power interconnect dollar content could
double on the next GPU platform, alongside market share gains; separate power racks
offer further potential depending on customer deployment. Optical diversification
includes fiber patch cords, active optical cables from 2H27, and CPO initiatives.
- Delta Electronics – Management sees potential for double-digit sequential revenue
growth in 4Q26 and stronger-than-seasonal performance in 1Q27. Component tightness is

likely to ease from 2Q27. High-voltage direct-current power rack shipments should start in
4Q26, initially with limited revenue contribution. Delta cited 80VDC as the current
reference design for the 2H27 GPU platform, although the design remains unfinalized.
- Lite-On – Management expects revenue growth of 5–10% QoQ in 4Q26, followed by
another single-digit increase in 1Q27, and reiterated its 70% cloud revenue growth target
for 2027. Power supplies, battery backup units and power racks should benefit from rising

AI cluster power consumption. Normalized gross/operating margin targets are 25–
30%/13–15%. Shipments of 800VDC power racks should begin in 3Q27.
Non-AI-driven consumer electronics
- Luxshare – Consumer electronics share gains, new AI devices and PC ODM development
offer growth opportunities. Luxshare also targets large-scale optical transceiver
production in 2027, complementing its electrical interconnect, liquid cooling and AI power
offerings.
- Sunny Optical – Sunny aims to sustain handset lens revenue despite industry weakness

and gain module share at Samsung and Huawei. Potential new product launches for a key
customer could support strong XR growth in 2027, while robotics offers a multi-year
optical demand opportunity. Passive optical interconnect components are undergoing
customer qualification.
- Transsion – 3Q26 is likely to mark the earnings trough; management targets positive
revenue and earnings growth in 2027. Memory inventories are normalizing, and
management considers further substantial memory price increases less likely. Higher

processor costs should have a relatively limited impact on the overall bill of materials.
Management targets energy storage revenue of Rmb1.5–2.0 billion in 2026 and more than

CHPT (6510.TWO)

3
CHPT
Valuation
We value CHPT using a PE-based approach that factors in earnings momentum and the structural shift towards high-end
testing. Our target price of NT$4,000 is derived from applying 35x to 2027E EPS, implying the company's average forward PE
since 2024 +1 SD, which we believe is justified given the company's entrenched position in the 2nm/3nm testing segment,
rising contribution from US AI GPU & AI ASIC projects, and the unique competitive advantage of its "All-in-House" vertical

integration model. With these structural drivers in place, and as we believe the market is starting to value it as an AI/HPC play
(rather than a consumer electronics stock), we expect the multiple to hold at a premium relative to its historical averages.
Risks
Our quant system rates the CHPT stock as High Risk for its share price volatility.
Key downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price, and to our earnings forecasts, include: 1) margin

compression from lower-than-expected utilization rates; 2) execution delays in concentrated US AI GPU and ASIC programs;
3) intensifying competition in testing boards from global peers; and 4) structural gross margin dilution from a shift towards
high-end burn-in boards.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

高階 ABF 展望維持正向  
◼ 3Q26 本業獲利符合預期，單季稅後 EPS 6.36 元：3Q26 營收
526.50 億元，+23%QOQ，+55%YOY，+38%累計 YOY；毛利率
28.85%，營益率 20%，本業獲利表現符合預期，業外收入 26.36 億
元，稅前淨利 131.63 億元，歸屬母公司稅後淨利 100.52 億元，
3Q26 單季稅後 EPS 6.36 元。 
◼ 高階 ABF 展望維持正向：展望後市，2027 年客戶產品 Roadmap 及
產能配置大致底定，且多已支付預付款，即便個別客戶需求出現變
化，亦可透過產能重新分配因應，整體高階 ABF 載板仍供不應求。
目前 AI 相關營收佔比達 70%，高階 ABF 受惠 AI 晶片尺寸及層數提
升，合理毛利率可達 50~60%。產能方面，光復二廠產能已全數獲
客戶預訂，欣興亦透過既有 BT 產線改造以緩解 ABF Core 產能瓶
頸，預估 2027~2028 年 ABF 產能每年增幅皆可達 20~30%。客戶透
過 LTA 及預付款方式確保長期高階產能，預期欣興未來幾年仍將受
惠高階產品佔比提升及產品組合優化。  
◼ 維持買進評等：預估 2026 年 EPS 為 23.49 元；隨生產工藝逐步提
升，欣興高階 ABF 產品合理毛利率為 50~60%，推估 2027 年 EPS
為 45.17 元，維持買進，目標價調整至 900 元(20x2027EPS)。

欣興為全球 IC 載板領導廠商  欣興電子為全球印刷電路板與 IC 載板製造領導廠商，屬於聯電旗下，總部位
於桃園龜山；產品涵蓋 ABF 載板、BT 載板、高密度連接板(HDI)、多層板、
FPC 及軟硬結合板等，廣泛應用於 AI 伺服器、高效能運算、網通設備、智慧
型手機、 消費性電子及車用電子等領域。目前生產基地位於台灣、中國、泰
國等地區， ABF/BT 載板、HDI 主要在台灣、中國生產，其中高階 ABF 廠區位
於新竹光復、 桃園楊梅，至於 PCB 主要在泰國進行生產。

2025 年產品營收佔比：Substrate 58%(ABF 46%+BT 12%)、HDI 27%、PCB 
11%、FPC 2%、其他 1%。預估 2026 年 Substrate 佔比 63%，+58%YOY， 
2026-2027 年成長以 ABF 為主，預期 2026 年 ABF 載板+74%YOY，BT 載板-
3%YOY，HDI 佔比 25%、PCB 佔比 9%、FPC 佔比 1%、其他佔比 1%。

3Q26 本業獲利符合預期，單季

高階 ABF 展望維持正向  展望後市，2027 年客戶產品 Roadmap 及產能配置大致底定，且多已支付預付
款，即便個別客戶需求出現變化，亦可透過產能重新分配因應，整體高階 ABF
載板仍供不應求。目前 AI 相關營收佔比達 70%，高階 ABF 受惠 AI 晶片尺寸
及層數提升，合理毛利率可達 50~60%。1) ABF：除既有 CoWoS 需求持續強
勁之外，EMIB-T 亦獲 Google TPU 以外的終端客戶關注，考量新平台開發將
排擠既有 CoWoS 相關載板產能，載板供應商 EMIB-T 產品毛利率要求更高，
預期未來設備配置將取決於 3 家載板廠(IBIDEN、Shinko、欣興)的良率及量產
能力；2)HDI: 3Q26 AI HDI 佔 HDI 約 50%、光模組佔約 30%，其餘 20%為其
他產品。

◼ 3Q26 營收大幅成長，產品組合優化推升毛利率創高：3Q26 營收
24.92 億元，+54%QOQ，+141%YOY，毛利率 43.2%，季增 7.2 個
百分點，創歷史新高，主要受惠產品漲價及高毛利產品占比提升，
1) 一般材料中 PP 黏合片營收占比提升，因製程較短、人工成本低
且無須使用銅箔，毛利率較一般 CCL 高；2) FR4 材料中高毛利率
HDI High-Tg產品占比增加，帶動一般材料產品組合優化。特殊材料
PI 產品今年營收成長 70%，毛利率近 70%。單季 EPS 6.0 元。 
◼ 特殊材料與高速產品持續放量，泰國廠及昆山廠擴產支撐 2027 年成
長：騰輝電子-KY 長期聚焦少量多樣、高毛利率利基型特殊材料市
場，憑藉自主樹脂合成及配方開發能力，持續拓展軍工航太、半導
體測試、AI 伺服器及高階散熱應用。產能方面，1) 泰國廠已啟動初
期生產，目前產能 3~5 萬張/月，預計 1Q27 少量供貨，預計增加
10%產能，2Q27 後將逐步放量；2) 至於昆山廠持續擴建並增設上
膠設備，預期提升 PP 黏合片產能 30~40%，以因應 HDI 及高階材
料需求成長，並支撐 2027 年成長 
◼ 維持買進評等：預估 2026/2027 年 EPS 分別為 19.84/44.62 元，維
持買進，目標價 580 元(13x2027EPS)。

騰輝電子-KY 產品主要分一般材
料與特殊材料

騰輝電子-KY 主要從事印刷電路板所需之黏合片、銅箔基板及鋁基板生產與銷
售，一般材料產品應用於光電板、汽車板等中低階產品，特殊材料主要為散熱
鋁基板，另外亦有著墨軍工航天等利基型領域，2025 年產品組合：一般材料
41%、特殊材料 53%、其他 6%；2026 年一般材料受惠漲價、缺貨，預估
2026 年產品組合：一般材料 58%、特殊材料 37%、其他 5%。騰輝總部位於
中國蘇州，主要生產基地位亦位於中國蘇州(月產能 80 萬張)、江陰(月產能 20
萬張)、深圳(月產能 10 萬張)與台灣(月產能 10 萬張)，泰國廠(月產能 10 萬張)
則於 2026 年 9 月開始量產。 
Exhibit 3:騰輝電子-KY 產品營收

Sources: Company、Taishin

3Q26 營收大幅成長，產品組合
優化推升毛利率創高 
 騰輝電子-KY 3Q26 營收 24.92 億元，+54%QOQ，+141%YOY，毛利率
43.2%，季增 7.2 個百分點，創歷史新高，主要受惠產品漲價及高毛利產品占
比提升，1) 一般材料中 PP 黏合片營收占比提升，因製程較短、人工成本低且
無須使用銅箔，毛利率較一般 CCL 高；2) FR4 材料中高毛利率 HDI High-Tg
產品占比增加，帶動一般材料產品組合優化。特殊材料方面，PI 產品今年營收
成長 70%，毛利率近 70%。費用方面，受惠營收規模擴大，3Q26 營業費用率
由 2Q26 的 19.2%降至 15.8%，季減 3.4 個百分點，帶動營業利益率由 16.8%
提升至 27.5%，季增 10.7 個百分點，3Q26 單季稅後 EPS 6.0 元。

特殊材料與高速產品持續放量，
泰國廠及昆山廠擴產支撐 2027
年成長 
 騰輝電子-KY 長期聚焦少量多樣、高毛利率利基型特殊材料市場，憑藉自主樹
脂合成及配方開發能力，持續拓展軍工航太、半導體測試、AI 伺服器及高階散
熱應用。產品方面，1) PI 材料：受惠軍工航太需求成長，以及 2 家韓系半導體
廠導入半導體探針板與老化板，PI 2026 年營收成長 70%，毛利率近 70%；2) 
不流膠 PP：隨同業逐步退出利基市場，訂單持續增加，目前營收占比 6%，預
期 2027 年提升至 10%以上，毛利率 60~70%；3) 散熱材料(散熱膜與基板)：
除既有車用照明市場外，亦積極拓展 AI 電源、高功率元件及散熱應用，高階
鋁基板單價可達一般鋁基板 4～5 倍；4) 高速材料：M7、M8 及 M9 等級產品
持續進行客戶認證，結合碳氫及 PTFE 材料技術，切入 GPU 伺服器、網通及
光模組等應用，預期 1Q27 逐步貢獻營收，目前高速材料占比仍低，2027 年預
期翻倍成長。

產能方面，目前整體稼動率仍有提升空間，惟原物料供應限制實際產出(目前
原物料取得率已自 60%提升至 80%)。1) 泰國廠已啟動初期生產，目前產能
3~5 萬張/月，預計 1Q27 少量供貨，預計增加 10%產能，2Q27 後將逐步放
量；2) 至 於 昆 山 廠 持 續 擴 建 並 增 設 上 膠 設 備 ， 預 期 提 升 PP 黏 合 片 產 能
30~40%，以因應 HDI 及高階材料需求成長，並支撐 2027 年成長。

자동차업종

◼ 3Q26 비용절감/환율 효과로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현 
◼ 2027년 물량–환율 영향 상쇄되며 2H27 이익체력 개선 본격화 예상 
◼ 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등에 주목

투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 50만원 하향(-6%) 
현대차 TP 50만원(시총 102조원)은 12MF EPS 41,978원에 Target PER 12배
적용하여 산출. 제한적인 실적 기대 반영해 타겟 밸류 하향함에 따라 TP 조정  
우려 대비 양호한 3Q26 실적으로 주가 하방 부담 완화. 주가의 추세적 상승 
위해서는 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등 확인돼야 할 
것. 기저효과에 따른 물량 개선이 주가 저점 받쳐줄 것이나, 현재 밸류(2027년 
PER 9.1배vs 글로벌 피어 6.1배)에서 추세 상승 이끌긴 어려울 것이기 때문. 
이에, 자동차 본업 외 1) 한/미국 Physical AI 투자 계획 및 2) BD 공급망 구축 
/양산 가시화에 따른 주가 반등 기대

3Q26 매출 45조(YoY -3%, 이하 YoY), OP 2.7조(+6%), OPM 5.9% 시현 
3Q26 OP 추정(2.5조)/컨센(2.4조) 상회. 파업에 따른 생산/판매 차질 영향으로 
기대 제한적이었으나, 비용 절감/환율 영향으로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현. 
[자동차] 매출 33조(-10%), OP 2.1조(+25%), OPM 6.5%. 도매 판매 92.1만대
(-11%). 판매 부진과 원자재 부담에도 불구, 환효과(+1.3조)/비용절감(+0.5조)
로 실적 방어. 이외 YoY 변수별 영향은 물량(-1.1조)/믹스(-0.6조)/원자재(-
0.5조). [금융] 매출 9.2조(+28%), OP 4,710억(-18%), OPM 5.1%, [기타] 매출 
2.9조(+2%), OP 712억(-72%), OPM 2.5% 기록

2027년 매출 191조(YoY +2%, 이하 YoY), OP 12.7조(+15%), OPM 6.6% 전망 
기저효과 바탕으로 연결 도매 물량 YoY +5% 증가 예상하나, 원달러 환율 -
4% 하락(가정 환율 1,380원)하며 1H27 → 2H27로가며 이익체력 개선 예상

### Page 5

DAISHIN SECURITIES

5  
  현대차 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스콜 QnA 
Q. 금융부문 환율 변동에 따른 4Q26 매출 및 손익 영향 
- 금융부문 환율 영향은 현대차 실적에 미치는 환율 영향과 동일한 방향으로 발생

Q. 3Q26 금융부문 수익성 악화 요인 
- 장기금리 상승에 따른 조달 및 이자비용 증가가 주요인. 자산/부채 만기 매칭에 따른 
금리 상승 영향 반영 
- EV 중심 중고차 가격지수 소폭 하락 및 차량 반환에 따른 매각손실, 금리 상승에 따
른 연체율 및 신용비용 상승 
- 차량 판매가격 인하 영향은 없으며, 3Q26 도매 판매 감소에 따른 금융 영업수익 영향 
불가피했음

Q. 향후 주주환원 정책에서 배당 및 자사주 매입 우선순위 
- 기존 밸류업 정책: 최소 DPS 1만원/TPR 35% 제시. 연간 배당 약 2.6조원 → TPR 
35% 충족 위한 잔여분 자사주 매입/소각 활용 
- 경영환경 변화 고려하여 기존 밸류업 프로그램 전반의 주주가치 제고 방안 재검토중 
- 새로운 정책 확정 및 주주 소통 전까지 기존 약속인 최소 DPS 1만원 유지할 계획 
- 시장의 배당 및 자사주 매입 간 균형 요구를 인지하고 있으며, 주주 의견 수렴 후 주
주가치 제고 계획 공유 예정

Q. 3Q26 영업이익 증감 요인 중 기타 +6,400억 발생 원인 
- 월별 컨틴전시 활동을 통한 비용 최적화 및 재료비 절감 지속 추진 
- 4Q26 원가 절감 효과는 3Q26 대비 확대 예상

Q. 3Q26 영업외손익 감소 원인 
- 3Q26 환율 급락으로 외화 매출채권 등 외화자산 관련 외화환산손실 및 외환거래손실 
발생 
- 매출채권 인식 시점과 회수 시점 간 환율 차이에 따른 외환차손 발생 
- 지분법 대상 기업들의 3Q26 실적 부진에 따른 지분법손익 감소도 일부 영향. 대부분 
외환 관련 손실에서 발생

Q. 중국 OEM 국내 진출 확대에 따른 대응 및 신차/가격 전략 
- 중국 OEM 및 테슬라의 국내 M/S 확대 대응중. 다만, 잦은 가격 조정은 중고차 잔존
가치 및 고객가치 훼손할 수 있어 지양 
- 현대캐피탈의 캡티브 금융 경쟁력 활용. 잔존가치 유예할부 등을 통해 월 납입금을 낮
춰 중국 OEM 대비 경쟁력 확보 
- 서비스 네트워크 기반으로 경쟁사 대비 경쟁력 확보 노력 
- 국내 신차: 4Q26 아반떼 HEV, 투싼, GV90, G80 HEV, 싼타페 FL(2026.12월), G90 
FL 등 출시 예정 
- 북미: 4Q26 투싼, GV80 HEV 출시 및 27년 초 GV90 출시 예상 
- 유럽: 아이오닉3 판매 개시, 4Q26 투싼/베이온 등 주요 모델 출시 예정 
- 중국: 아이오닉 V / 인도: 베이온, 아이오닉 P 등 출시 예정 
- 신차 출시 계획 연도별로 다수 예정. 가격은 원가/경쟁사/소비자 가격 수용성 등을 고
려해 결정

Q. 3Q26 법인세비용 증가 원인 및 4Q26 전망 
- 일회성 요인보다는 법인별 손익 믹스 및 적용 세율 차이에 따른 영향 
- 4Q26 법인세 수준은 QoQ 큰 변동 없을 것으로 전망

## Document 226: [012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 3Q26 Re_ 쇼크에 가려진 수주 성과한화에어로스페이스(012450) 3Q26 Re_ 쇼크에 가려진 수주 성과3Q26 Re_ 쇼크_16e51ae9

 상승여력 95%

 외인지분율 45.16%  
 배당수익률 0.74%  
 주요주주 한화 외 6 인

주가수익률 (%) 1개월 3개월 12개월  
 절대 (24.9) (6.2) (23.5)  
 상대 (13.1) (10.5) (47.4)  
 절대 (달러환산) (23.8) 1.5  (18.0)

3Q26 Review: “조삼모사” Again  
3Q26P 연결 실적은 매출액 6조 943억원(YoY -6.0%), 영업이익 4,520억원(YoY -46.2%, OPM 
7.4%)을 기록하며 컨센서스 대비 연결 매출액(7.5조원)과 영업이익(1.16조원) 모두 대폭 하회했다. 주
요 자회사가 양호한 실적을 기록했음에도 불구하고, 지상방산 실적이 매우 부진했다. 지상방산은 매출
액 1조 2,455억원(YoY -41.0%), 영업이익 217억원(YoY -96.2%, OPM 1.7%)을 기록했는데, 1)3Q 
생산 물량이 4Q 매출 인식되는 흐름 속, 고정비 부담이 컸었고, 2)낮은 수출 비중(3Q 48%, 2Q 58%)
과 국내외 사업의 부진한 믹스(국내 BEP 수준, 해외 OPM 11% 추정), 3)비우호적인 원/달러 환율, 4)
대전 사업장 사고 복구 및 기타 일회성 비용(300억원+160억원, 총 460억원) 등이 반영되었다.   
대전 사업장 정상화 시점과 사업 차질 영향은? 
대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 중 정상화될 예정이며, 보은과 여수 공장에서 생산 보완을 위한 조치가 진
행 중인 것으로 소통했다. 또한 대전 사업장은 국내 물량 위주이며, 수출 납기(천무, 천궁-II 추진체 
등)에는 지장이 없을 것이라고 언급했지만, 기타 사업장 램프업을 위한 인건비 증가 등, 수익성 악화는 
불가피할 것으로 예상된다.  
15%대의 매출 성장 가이던스는 달성이 가능할까? 
보수적으로 접근하는 것이 합리적이라는 판단이다. 당사는 ‘26E 지상방산 매출액을 일부 ‘27~28E로 
이연하면서 -6.1% 하향 조정했다. 사측은 폴란드 EC1 천무 인도 가이던스를 기존 40문에서 60문으
로 상향 조정하는 등 납품 속도가 예정대비 빨라진 것을 언급했다. 다만, 천무 EC1에서 미사일 물량 
비중이 높은 것으로 파악되기 때문에 생산 지연 가능성과 수출입 데이터 상 12월 출하 물량의 매출 인
식 이월 가능성을 반영했다. ‘27E는 폴란드 후속 사업과 루마니아 K9, 호주 레드백 등이 양산 페이즈
에 진입하며 성장세가 지속될 전망이다.    
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 1,480,000원 유지 
실적 쇼크와는 별개로, 미국 K9MH 시제, 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등 수주는 서프라이즈라고 판
단된다. 분기말 수주잔고는 35조원(수출 비중 74%, 스페인 미포함)으로 중장기 성장성에 대한 이견은 
없다. 불가피한 분기 실적 변동으로 동사의 미래 가치를 예단하는 것은 시기상조라는 판단이다.

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Setting the Stage for 2027
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Typically, a new administration sets the stage in their first year with stimulative policy
changes that play out in the second year after the new policies have been enacted into
law and begin to have their impact. This pattern shows up in historical data with the
average rate of gross domestic product (GDP) growth highest in the second year of

presidential terms compared to the other three years of the presidential term. At the
same time, equity market returns tend to be lowest on average in the second year of
presidential terms, which seems to be at odds with the fact that growth is strongest.
This apparent inconsistency can be attributed to the fact that the stock market is a
leading indicator that anticipates stronger growth and higher profits well before they
actually arrive. Once they do, as they have in 2026, the markets are looking ahead to the

inevitable slowing from the policy-induced peak in growth that typically arrives after the
midterm election year. The midterms also create uncertainty about the subsequent policy
environment which damps stocks ahead of the election only to be reversed on average
once the elections are over and the outlook is clarified. This phenomenon is seen in the
fact that on average the stock market has corrected about 20% ahead of midterms only
to rise about 30% from that bottom in the year after.

Q3 GDP data illustrate this pattern. Economic growth picked up through the first three
quarters of 2026 to a peak of 3.4%. Consumer spending growth averaged 3.8% over the
past six months, while the AI investment boom accelerated at a midteens growth rate.
Private sector purchases were growing at just shy of a 5% growth rate. GDP growth is
lower than that because imports of Asia produced technology-gear for the AI buildout
are subtracting a good bit from that underlying 5% domestic demand growth pace.

The new policies aimed at helping consumers through lower taxes and investment
incentives to spur private sector production have boosted growth as expected. Still, this
acceleration is passing, and the fiscal boost is likely to fade in 2027. Profits naturally
have followed this stronger GDP growth higher.
The upward trend in analysts' earnings revisions has started to roll over in our view, and,
while earnings are still growing, the rate of growth is likely to slow from here. In simple

terms, the second derivative of growth has turned negative. From way above trend
toward trend growth is still sufficient for the bull market to continue.
Another aspect of this transition to midcycle aside from the fading midterm
election boost from fiscal measures is the shift of monetary policy from maximum
accommodation to a more neutral policy. The Fed's rate cuts helped the midterm
acceleration in growth. Its shift to removing accommodation with rate hikes this year

adds to the odds of some momentum loss in 2027. That's already apparent as Federal
Reserve (Fed) Chair Warsh noted in his press conference after the September Federal
Open Market Committee meeting. Weaker balance sheet borrowers have seen their
spreads blow out as credit becomes more expensive. So far, the damage is confined to
the lowest-quality borrowers. Before, these spreads were unusually low reflecting easy
money conditions that are now normalizing. It's still early days in the new credit cycle.

Of course, how the market responds to this new Fed tightening cycle depends on how
aggressively the Fed raises rates. Analysts at Ned Davis Research note the S&P 500
has gained an average of 4.4% in the first year of all postwar tightening cycles. This
outcome varies considerably depending on the extent of the tightening. Aggressive
tightening cycles have seen an average decline of 3.6% a year later, whereas gradual
tightenings have seen a 10.5% average gain. In short, as one might expect, too much

tightening can cause a decline in stocks. Currently, the market seems to anticipate a
Investment Implications
Equities should continue to outperform
bonds in 2027 in our view, but
performance is likely to shift in favor of
higher-quality, less leveraged segments
of the market as financial conditions
tighten and earnings momentum slows,
while the accelerating momentum of
the early cycle transitions to midcycle
cruise control.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
December and March nudge up in rates to reverse the easings by the Powell Fed. This
would be consistent with an ongoing equity bull market in 2027. Obviously, inflation
will have a lot to say about whether a mild or aggressive tightening cycle is called for.
With wage pressures benign, productivity strong and the early-cycle acceleration set to
peak, we believe the odds of an aggressive tightening cycle that derails the bull market

are low.
Other policy effects have shaped the current economic cycle. The shift toward more
nationalistic trade policies with tariffs, for example, has drastically impacted the pattern
of trade flows. U.S. imports from China have dropped from about 20% 10 years ago to
less than 10%, according to research from Strategas. Nevertheless, contrary to concerns
of a trade-induced recession, overall trade has continued to grow in line with the global
economy. Rather than deglobalization, the world is undergoing a reglobalization into new

trade spheres that better align with national interests.
According to McKinsey Quarterly, "The 2025 U.S. National Security Strategy, for
example, reinforces economic and supply chain security, industrial capacity, technological
leadership, and capital market strength as national priorities. China's latest five-year plan,
meanwhile, includes technological self-reliance, economic stability and the modernization
of core industries among its key pillars. In this environment, value creation is increasingly

shaped by new trade corridors, growing defense spending, expansion of export controls
and industrial policies, and shifting flows of foreign investment."1 These new trade
policies are an impetus for growth that is structural, helping to make for a strong
investment cycle.
Obviously, AI is a big spur for investment that has been facilitated by the new policies
such as accelerated depreciation of capital expenditures (capex). McKinsey Research
designates 18 arenas with cutting-edge industries they believe are driving the global

growth story for the next couple of decades. They note companies headquartered in
the U.S. and China account for about 90% of these arenas' market value today. U.S.
companies account for 75% of the market capitalization within the 18 arenas and
generate about 50% of revenues. Given its leading role in the arenas driving growth
into the future, we believe the U.S. is well poised to outperform as the world moves
into this new technological future. The 18 arenas are part of the economy growing by

several multiples of the old economy segments and help explain why U.S. Equities have a
persistent valuation premium over the rest of the world.
Because of the unusually rapid growth of these new technologies associated with the
singularity, while earnings growth may decelerate from its peak, there are structural
reasons to believe earnings are on a new, higher-growth trajectory and unlikely to revert
fully back to historical norms. The new technologies have much higher margins than the

old economy sectors. The combination of faster growth and higher margins bodes well
for the equity market as these new arenas of future economic growth become a bigger
part of the U.S. and global economies.
The midterms have changed views about the relative performance of some segments
of the economy. For example, strong sentiment to slow the buildout of AI data centers
was dramatically evident in the election results. Beneficiaries of the buildout are being

revalued to reflect this slower growth. Disapproval of the Iran War was also registered
in the results. Energy prices have dropped on hopes for a quicker end to the war. This in
turn has lowered inflation expectations and interest rates. Overall, while the midterms
have remixed relative performance between sectors they have not changed the outlook
for continued solid growth and an ongoing bull market in equities.
1 McKinsey Quarterly, Q3 2026, page 52.

### Page 4

4 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
China, the WTO and the Great Convergence No One Expected
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Twenty-five years ago this December, the West made one of the biggest economic bets
of the modern era: It brought China into the WTO, convinced that membership would
transform the communist giant into a more open and market-oriented economy.
The logic seemed impeccable at the time. The Soviet Union had long been vanquished.

American capitalism appeared triumphant. Free trade, deregulation, privatization and
unfettered cross-border flows of goods, services, capital and labor were widely viewed as
essential ingredients of economic prosperity. Globalization ruled.
Fold China into the rules-based system, the thinking went, and economic liberalization
would gradually pull Beijing toward Western norms. China would become a willing
participant in the U.S.-led global economic order. The world would change China, in

other words.
Not quite. At the dawn of the 25th anniversary of China’s WTO accession, the great irony is
not how China became more like the West. It is how the West became more like China.
In the U.S., and across Europe and Asia, governments are increasingly embracing the
elements of China’s economic playbook, which range from state subsidies to the promotion
of national champions to strategic industrial policies, among other initiatives antithetical to

the global economic norms that prevailed when China joined the WTO.
The deployment of trade tariffs, the subsidization of semiconductor production, the
promotion of domestic manufacturing, and the screening of foreign investment are no
longer hallmarks of China alone. They are increasingly features of economic policymaking
in the U.S., the one-time champion of globalization. The European Union, Japan, South
Korea and other nations that once carried the flag for free trade and open markets have

moved much in the same direction. Even the World Bank—a chief architect and guardian of
Western-led globalization—has come around to blessing the merits of industrial policy, a
strategy central to China’s economic ascent.
To be sure, Washington has not embraced China’s economic model wholesale. Neither have
Brussels, Tokyo or Seoul. The political, economic and institutional differences between
these systems and China’s remain vast. But the direction—set by China—is unmistakable.

It was not supposed to be this way. To gain admission to the WTO, China made significant
concessions over 15 years of negotiations. Beijing slashed tariffs on industrial goods and
agricultural products. Various sectors—manufacturing, distribution, telecommunications,
banking and autos—were gradually opened to foreign firms. Commercial laws and regulations
were amended or written, including rules governing intellectual property rights. Nontariff
barriers like quotas, import licenses, dual pricing and opaque industry standards were either

eliminated or phased out. State-owned enterprises were reformed and restructured.
These concessions—and more—were taken as signs that China was serious about
becoming a global stakeholder. It stoked optimism among senior U.S. officials that China
was inevitably bound to converge and embrace Western economic norms. And, for a while,
the bet seemed to pay off.
As China lowered trade and investment barriers at the beginning of the century, U.S. goods

exports soared. American firms poured capital into the mainland, attracted by an enormous
labor pool, lower production costs and what would soon become one of the largest
consumer markets in the world.
China’s massive labor pool-cum-ballooning middle class paid off handsomely for Corporate
America. U.S. affiliate income earned in China rose from just $1.6 billion (B) in 2001 to
nearly $9B by 2010, making China one of the most profitable markets in the world for U.S.

goods and services. American consumers, meanwhile, enjoyed the benefits of low-cost
China imports. And debt-laden America benefited as China recycled its excess savings into
U.S. Treasurys, helping to grease the financial wheels of America.
Investment Implications
We expect China will continue to rely
on exports and investment to drive
growth amid weak consumption and
domestic demand. Manufacturing-
heavy economies like Germany
remain particularly exposed,

reinforcing our neutral stance toward
Europe in portfolios.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
But just as WTO accession helped boost China’s imports, it also supercharged China’s
export machine. Rather than shrink, as some optimists had predicted, America’s
merchandise trade deficit with China more than doubled between 2001 ($83.2B) and 2005
($202.8B) and continued rising thereafter. As the decade wore on, U.S. manufacturing jobs
and wages came under intense pressure owing to expanding China imports and the shift

in U.S. production to lower-cost China. Entire supply chains migrated across the Pacific,
decimating many manufacturing communities across the U.S.
China began producing and exporting at a scale the world had never experienced. In the
span of just a decade, China’s share of world manufacturing output soared from 8.7%
in 2000 to 18.1% in 2010, when the nation eclipsed the U.S. as the world’s largest
manufacturer of goods. Four years later, China was outproducing all of Europe—and has

never looked back (Exhibit 1A).
Today, China sits at the center of global manufacturing and trade, competing not simply in
textiles, toys and other low-value products but also in more technology-driven industries
like electric vehicles, drones, machinery and clean energy. China now accounts for roughly
28% of global manufacturing, according to the World Bank, well ahead of second place
America (17%). Meanwhile, the nation’s share of world exports—just 4% in 2001—now

stands at 15%, roughly on par with the combined market share of second-place America
and Germany (Exhibit 1B). The world is so awash with China goods that the nation is on
course to post another annual merchandise trade surplus in excess of $1 trillion.
Not surprisingly, China’s grip on world trade has significantly dimmed the brimming
optimism that greeted the nation’s WTO entry a quarter-century ago. Today, the consensus
is that China’s trade surplus has been aided and abetted by nonmarket practices and

policies that include forced technology transfers, currency manipulations, state subsidies
to industry, restrictions on foreign investment in strategic sectors, intellectual property
infringements and resource protectionism. Rather than dismantle its state-led, nonmarket
and mercantilist economy, China has tacked in the other direction, notably under the
leadership of Xi Jinping.
For investors, the implications have been profound. We now live in a world where

governments increasingly decide which industries are strategically indispensable, which
technologies must remain domestically controlled and which companies require public
financing. The “Commanding Heights”—or who controls the economy—have shifted
from the private sector to the public sector. In this world, investment returns are not only
determined by traditional metrics like earnings, the cost of capital and valuations but also
by geopolitics, energy costs, trade protectionism and national security. The world today is

far different from the world when China joined the WTO.
Suffice it to say that the 25th anniversary of China’s entry into the WTO won’t be met
with much celebration in the West. The consensus in 2001 was that the world would
change China. Twenty-five years later, it’s China that has changed the world. It’s the great
convergence no one saw coming.

6 of 8 November 9, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are we reviewing to gauge the capital investment needed for the proposed Artificial
Intelligence buildout and the return required to cover its costs?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
Capex by the 15 largest data center developers are expected to top $1.4 trillion in
2027, which is somewhere between 3.5% and 4% of U.S. GDP. A large portion of the
capital investment is earmarked for compute. The total capacity of gigawatts (GW)

under construction is around 34GW of Informaton Technology capacity, with the U.S.
accounting for approximately 70% of this, according to BloombergNEF. Given the
massive compute investment, we continue to analyze closely the total debt financing
announced, the approximate cost of capital of the capital providers, the associated
revenues needed to match or exceed cost estimates, and the individual credit spreads
(as well as credit default swaps spreads) of the larger hyperscalers and chip makers to

better assess the overall return on investment of the AI buildout.
BloombergNEF estimates, that at the beginning of this year, the highest-rated data
center bonds offered yields below 5%, which is now closer to around 7% today. In
addition, AI-tied firms have raised over $460B in debt financing this year, which is
more than double that from 2025. Finally, according to BloombergNEF and Bloomberg
Intelligence, as of September 18, 2026, it is estimated that the 15 largest publicly listed

data center companies would need to produce $2.5 trillion in revenues in 2032 in order
to achieve a return on invested capital equal to the company-level weighted average cost
of capital. This is about 5 times more than the expected revenue in 2026.
Recently, the concern across the capital markets is that there is not enough financing
capital to go around in order to fund the expected buildout, which is one of the primary
reasons why credit spreads have widened and yields have risen. We view this as a natural

development of such large-scale infrastructure transformations. This is the reason why
we continue to watch the overall health of balance sheets of the builders, the level of
profitability and margins, and the order backlogs/cancellations in the next few years. We
still believe we are closer to the middle of the AI buildout than to the end. Therefore, we
view larger-scale weakness in the higher-quality segments of the AI chain as potential
buying opportunities for investors who are underexposed to these areas.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

## Document 336: French economy sinks deeper into gloom

Source PDF: all report pdf/French economy sinks deeper into gloom.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-27
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

France’s third-quarter GDP contraction was deeper than
previously estimated, conﬁrming the sharp deterioration in the
economy. Domestic demand shows no sign of recovery and
the labour market is weakening. We now expect growth of just
0.1% in 2026 and 0.4% in 2027. The only good news is that
inﬂationary pressures are easing
Charlotte de Montpellier
We now expect growth of just 0.1% in France this year
Domestic demand no longer supports
growth
The second estimate of French GDP conﬁrmed the economy's

deterioration. GDP contracted 0.2% quarter-on-quarter in the third
quarter, compared with the initially reported 0.1% decline. The
unrounded data also shows that GDP edged down by 0.02% in the
second quarter, although the rounded ﬁgure pointed to stagnation.
Following a 0.2% decline in the ﬁrst quarter, the French economy
has therefore contracted for three consecutive quarters. It has

### Page 2

technically been in recession since the beginning of the year.
Compared with a year earlier, GDP was also down 0.2%.
The downward revision mainly reﬂected weaker construction
investment and softer activity in information and communication
services. Looking at the details, household consumption increased
by 0.3%, after 0.2% in the second quarter. Government
consumption rose at the same pace. But investment contracted
sharply again, falling by 0.9% after a 0.5% decline. This reﬂected

the continued downturn in construction and a fall in investment in
manufactured goods. Overall, ﬁnal domestic demand excluding
inventories stagnated and made no contribution to growth. Its
contribution had initially been estimated at 0.1 percentage points.
The sharp fall in exports was the main reason for the GDP
contraction. Exports dropped by 1.6%, after rising by 2.9% in the
previous quarter, while imports edged up by 0.1%. Net trade
therefore subtracted 0.6 percentage points from growth. Inventories

moved in the opposite direction, adding 0.3 percentage points.
Without this support, the contraction would have been much
deeper.
Growth is likely to reach just 0.1% in 2026
Following this revision, the annual growth carry-over for 2026
stands at only 0.1%. It is very diﬃcult to see domestic demand
rebounding strongly enough in the fourth quarter to improve this
result. Business and consumer conﬁdence both deteriorated in
October and November. A weakening labour market and growing

fears of unemployment will keep households cautious. At the same
time, weak demand and a lack of visibility will continue to weigh on
business investment.
Today’s consumption ﬁgures do little to change this assessment.
Household spending on goods stopped falling in October, but
merely stagnated after a 0.9% decline in September. This is not a
recovery. Against this backdrop, net trade is probably the only
factor that can prevent another GDP contraction in the fourth

quarter. After its very poor third-quarter performance, its
contribution should at least become less negative.

### Page 3

We therefore expect zero growth in the fourth quarter. This would
leave average GDP growth at just 0.1% in 2026, well below the 1.0%
we expect for the eurozone.
France will enter 2027 without momentum
The outlook for 2027 remains very gloomy. Salaried employment
fell by 94,400 jobs over the past year, a decline of 0.3%. The
situation is even worse in the private sector, where 109,000 jobs
were lost over the same period, a fall of 0.5%. The weakening

labour market will increasingly weigh on household conﬁdence,
income and consumption. Financing conditions also remain
diﬃcult, while uncertainty over future taxes and public spending is
exceptionally high. Both businesses and households are likely to
keep postponing some projects.
It is therefore diﬃcult to identify a driver capable of generating a
meaningful recovery in domestic demand over the next few
quarters. The third-quarter contraction and the likely absence of a

fourth-quarter rebound also leave a very weak starting point for
2027. France will enter next year without economic momentum.
Even if quarterly growth turns slightly positive again, this will not be
enough to produce solid annual growth. We expect GDP to grow by
just 0.4% on average in 2027, with risks still tilted to the downside.
Lower inﬂation is the only good news
The only good news in this very weak outlook is that the risk of an
inﬂationary spiral remains low. Headline inﬂation remains too high

for comfort but fell to 2.9% in November from 3.4% in October.
Harmonised inﬂation, the measure relevant for the European
Central Bank, also fell, from 3.7% to 3.2%.
The decline was mainly driven by energy. Energy inﬂation slowed
to 19.2% year-on-year from 24.1% in October, reﬂecting a marked
slowdown in petroleum product prices. Services inﬂation also
declined, from 2.6% to 2.3%, as inﬂation in accommodation and
transport services eased. This conﬁrms that the acceleration seen in

October was temporary rather than the beginning of a broader
increase in domestic price pressures.

Food inﬂation edged up from 1.2% to 1.3%, mainly because of
fresh fruit and other fresh food products, whose prices rose by 9.4%

year-on-year. Prices for other food products were almost
unchanged. Manufactured goods prices continued to fall, declining
by 0.2% compared with a year earlier.
Underlying inﬂationary pressures therefore remain much weaker
than headline inﬂation suggests. Weak demand and the
deterioration in the labour market are limiting wage pressures and
companies’ ability to raise prices. In an economy that has virtually
ground to a halt, a broad-based resurgence in inﬂation looks very

unlikely.

## Document 339: Global Economics Analyst_ Macro Outlook 2027_ Steady Baseline, Downside Risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Economics Analyst_ Macro Outlook 2027_ Steady Baseline, Downside Risks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 30
Extraction status: ok

### Page 2

Macro Outlook 2027: Steady Baseline, Downside Risks

We entered 2026 expecting sturdy global growth and slower inflation. The war in Iran 
derailed this inflation forecast, but the global economy proved more resilient than 
feared at the start of the war. 
Even though global headline inflation is on course to end 2026 at 3.7% on a Q4/Q4 
basis—1.3pp higher than our forecast from last year—we estimate that the global 
economy still grew at a remarkably unremarkable 2.7%, in line with potential and only 
0.1pp below our projections from our 2026 outlook (Exhibit 1). Our interpretation is that 
surging AI capex, easier financial conditions, and (in the US, Germany, and parts of Asia) 
fiscal expansion mostly offset the energy supply shock. As a result of these tailwinds, 
GDP growth only modestly underperformed in the US (-0.3pp), Euro area (-0.3pp), and 
China (-0.2pp) while significantly outperforming in Asian economies such as Taiwan 
(+8.6pp) and Korea (+1.9pp) that benefited from surging AI exports.

Growth Remains Solid but Unremarkable in 2027 
We expect another year of solid but unremarkable GDP growth in 2027, but several of 
the impulses that shaped global growth in 2026 will look different. 
The headwind from the energy supply shock will likely flip next year as energy prices 
normalize. Our commodity strategists’ baseline is that continued Middle East supply 
adaptation will lower Brent prices to $78/bbl by end-2027 with a similar normalization 
in global LNG prices (Exhibit 2). Based on these projections, we forecast that the energy 
price impulse to global growth will swing from a 0.4pp drag on global growth in 2026 to 
a modest boost in 2027 (although we see a continued drag in Europe from delayed 
energy cost pass-through to consumers).

Exhibit 1: Inflation Surged, but Growth Proved Resilient in 2026

### Page 3

A positive growth impulse from energy prices should outweigh the effect of tighter 
financial conditions and a smaller fiscal impulse in 2027. Our GS Financial Conditions 
Index (GS FCI) has tightened by around 50bp in the US and by 30bp in developed 
markets (DMs) over the last three months on the back of lower equity prices and higher 
rates. It now implies a moderate drag on growth over the next few quarters (Exhibit 3). 
We do not expect meaningful fiscal consolidation owing to higher rates in 2027, but the 
large fiscal expansions of the last few years are fading. The main exception is Japan, 
where consumption tax cuts, an increase in defense spending, and the Takaichi 
administration’s investment plans point to a meaningful 0.7pp boost to GDP growth. We 
also expect modestly positive impulses in Germany and the US—where the Democratic 
victory in November’s US election suggests some of the spending cuts from the 2025 
One Big Beautiful Bill Act will be reversed—but a continued fiscal drag in the UK that 
leaves the DM fiscal impulse only slightly positive.

### Page 4

The main global growth impulse that we do not expect to change in 2027 is increased AI 
capex. We forecast that nominal global AI investment will rise from $1.1tn in 2026 to 
$1.8tn in 2027, mostly driven by an increase of over $500bn in hardware spending in the 
US (Exhibit 4). We expect the investment surge to be frontloaded, with the 
year-over-year change in investment as a share of GDP peaking in 2027H1 (the level of 
investment as a share of GDP peaks in 2028 under our forecast). Even with the expected 
slowdown, the impact on GDP growth will be sizable: we estimate that AI investment will 
boost GDP growth by 0.4pp globally. And although we again estimate only a small 0.2pp 
boost to measured US GDP growth in 2027 owing to continued undercounting of 
semiconductor investment and chip-related intellectual property exports, we estimate a 
larger 0.6pp boost to “true” GDP growth after correcting for these measurement biases.

The other notable growth impulse from 2026 that will continue in 2027 is a drag on 
rest-of-the-world growth from China. Our forecasts for China remain above consensus 
for 2027 (4.5% GS vs. 4.4% consensus), but this again reflects very robust expectations 
for Chinese exports and a further increase in China’s trade surplus (Exhibit 5). By 
contrast, we expect domestic demand growth to remain weak in 2027 owing to a 1.3pp 
of GDP property sector drag and weak underlying consumer spending, resulting in a 
modest 0.1pp headwind to GDP growth for the rest of the world, with larger growth 
drags in manufacturing-heavy economies such as Germany (-0.3pp) and Central Europe 
(-0.2pp).

### Page 6

Taken together, our 2027 global GDP growth forecast is modestly optimistic at 2.8%, a 
pace that is 0.1pp above our estimate of potential. As has been the case for the last 
several years, we are more optimistic on US growth (2.4% vs. 2.2% consensus) owing to 
resilient investment and more downbeat on the European growth outlook (0.9% vs. 
1.3%) owing to tighter financial conditions and our expectation that higher energy 
prices will start to bite this winter. We also expect continued robust growth in South 
Korea (3.2% vs. 2.7%) and Taiwan (6.3% vs. 5.9%) reflecting another year of strong AI 
exports.

Downside Risks 
We see risks around our baseline global growth forecasts as skewed to the downside for 
three reasons. 
1. A Renewed Energy Shock 
The first and most immediate risk is that an escalation of the war in Iran could lead to 
more significant energy price increases, creating growth headwinds that—unlike in 
2026—FCI and fiscal impulses would not offset in 2027. While our commodity strategists 
see risks around their energy price baselines as two-sided, they put more weight on an 
upside scenario where intensified shipping attacks, damage to oil infrastructure, and a 
more persistent risk premium push oil prices up to $132/bbl (Exhibit 7). If these risks 
materialize, the energy price impulse would remain a moderate drag on global GDP 
growth in 2027.

2. Bond Market Turbulence 
Second, we worry about a bigger fiscal risk premium. In 2026, we saw the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—show 
up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates. Although rates have eased 
somewhat over the last few weeks, the DM yield curve remains 85bp above its level in 
early 2026. If sustained, we estimate that this increase would subtract a cumulative 0.5% 
from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment (Exhibit 
8). Only part of this drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect 
bond yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.

Exhibit 7: A Further Rise in Oil Prices Owing to Middle East Escalation Could Derail the Growth Outlook

3. A Weaker AI Impulse
The transition to an AI-enabled economy is occurring in two phases. The first phase
involves an investment boom that raises both corporate profits and global GDP growth
but features only limited productivity gains and labor market disruption (because it
takes firms several years to adopt AI and reorganize to take full advantage of the new
technology). The second phase involves lower hardware investment but more visible
business reorganization, as well as more substantial productivity gains and labor market

disruption.
To illustrate this transition, we overlay a simulated AI capex path with our estimates of
the labor market impacts under a 10-year adoption timeline (Exhibit 10). The capex
path assumes that US AI investment follows equity analysts’ projections through 2032,
after which the capital stock converges toward a long-run equilibrium level.1  Under our
baseline calibration, the AI capital stock eventually converges to a long-run level of
around 9% of GDP, and 9% of total US workers are displaced. (We continue to expect

most of these displaced workers to find new jobs, including in new occupations that are
created by AI).
Under these assumptions, the transition from the first phase to the second phase
doesn’t occur until 2028-2029, when we see a further pickup in the underlying US
productivity trend to the 2½-3% range and a gradual increase in the US unemployment
rate to 4½%. A backloaded transition is consistent with the projections by equity
analysts that AI investment will keep growing through 2028, albeit at a slower pace than

in 2026-2027. It is also consistent with the renewed pickup in US employment growth
since the spring, which has temporarily laid to rest concerns around imminent AI-related
job market disruption.
1 We characterize the long-run equilibrium level following Hall and Jorgenson (1967) as a function of the
depreciation rate, technological cost disinflation, nominal interest rates, and the AI capital factor share of total
output.
12 November 2026   9
Goldman Sachs Global Economics Analyst

### Page 10

But it is hard to be sure about the timing of this shift and we see some signs that it could 
occur earlier.  Hyperscaler equity price reactions to earnings call signals for higher capex 
switched from positive in 2024-2025 to slightly negative in 2026 as the market has 
become concerned about overinvestment (Exhibit 11). Furthermore, upward revisions to 
hyperscaler capex slowed in the Q3 earnings season, which suggests that the AI 
investment cycle peak is shifting more clearly into view.

Exhibit 10: AI Investment Is Frontloaded, but AI Labor Market Disruption Is Backloaded

### Page 11

Beyond the fundamental demand for compute, we see three other near-term downside 
risks for AI investment: 
Capacity constraints – Semiconductor and memory producers are capacity n
constrained with order backlogs through 2028. While capacity investments currently 
underway should eventually alleviate these constraints, a surge in nominal 
investment in 2027 may increasingly raise prices (thereby pushing central banks 
toward more hawkish policy) without boosting real output. 
Credit markets – Global AI-related debt issuance has totaled $687bn so far in 2026 n
while AI-related equity issuance has added another $115bn. While our market 
strategists see adequate capital supply to meet even higher capital demand next 
year, upward pressure on borrowing costs could lead to more meaningful crowd-out 
of other investment in 2027 (vs. only modest crowd-out in 2026). 
Regulation – Concerns regarding regulatory headwinds to investment surged after n
the US midterm elections in early November raised the possibility of data center 
permitting moratoria in several states (particularly in Texas). We do not anticipate 
that regulation will derail 2027 investment, both because moratoria will likely be 
short-lived and because permits for 2027 projects have mostly already been issued. 
But a more extended delay or stringent regulatory shift would raise downside risks to 
AI investment in 2028-2029, which could weigh on growth in 2027 via tighter 
financial conditions. 
Turning to the labor market, we have argued that AI adoption is likely to displace jobs 
and cause a temporary increase in the unemployment rate of around ½ a percentage 
point over the next several years.2  Our baseline expectation is that this increase occurs 
in a backloaded fashion, with only a small effect in 2027 and more notable effects in 
2028-2029.  However, we are starting to see signs that the AI impact is spreading 
2  See “Revisiting the Extent and Timing of AI Labor Displacement,” Global Economics Analyst, October 22, 
2026.

beyond technology to the broader economy. Across nonmanufacturing industries, the 
share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment (correlations were insignificant at the 
start of the year, Exhibit 12).

Moreover, AI-driven job displacement would likely accelerate in a downside growth 
scenario. Exhibit 13 shows that the trend decline in the employment share of routine 
occupations over the past five decades has been highly concentrated in recessions.  
Given that AI has sharply increased the number of occupations exposed to automation, 
we would expect corporate leaders to respond to a slowdown by reducing headcount 
targets more aggressively, putting more upward pressure on the unemployment rate.

### Page 13

To be clear, we still expect AI-related pressure on the labor market to remain temporary.  
Employment in existing industries such as healthcare is likely to keep growing rapidly, 
and history shows that technological revolutions ultimately result in the creation of new 
industries and occupations that absorb workers displaced elsewhere.  Historically, this 
dynamism has kept the unemployment rate from increasing permanently despite all the 
labor-saving technological innovations of the past 250 years.  But over the next few 
years, job market disruption is likely to grow.  
Inflation: Progress Not Perfection 
Inflation progress was slower than expected in 2026 owing to price increases associated 
with the war in Iran.  But under our baseline commodity forecasts for a steady decline in 
energy prices next year, inflation should slow in 2027. 
The case for disinflation is particularly strong in the US because the price boosts from 
tariffs (which are still adding 40bp to year-over-year core PCE inflation through 
October) should fully drop out of the year-over-year inflation statistics next spring 
(Exhibit 14). Combined with a much smaller inflation boost from the war in Iran, we 
expect core PCE to slow from 3.2% at end-2026 to 2.4% at end-2027.

Exhibit 13: Prior Automation Cycles Suggest That Aggregate Automation Speeds Up in

Despite our expectation for progress, we see several reasons why inflation may remain 
somewhat firm in early 2027, thereby delaying a return to target-consistent levels in 
most economies. 
First, while most of the price boost from higher energy prices has been realized, some 
pass-through will spill into next year (Exhibit 15).  This is true both in the US and 
especially in Europe, where indirect effects have so far been very limited.  For example, 
European airfares have not repriced higher in response to fuel cost increases so far—
reflecting fuel hedges, greater competition, and pre-war plans for supply 
expansion—but should pick up notably next year.
